# Verb pass report, Stage 5 (2026-10-02)

Generated by `corpus/working/build_report.py --pass-dir corpus/working/verb_pass/stage5` from the Stage 5 check results and skeptic verdicts; see Stage 5 of `prompts/verb-pass-plan.md`. Do not edit by hand: re-run the script. Decisions go in `corpus/working/verb_pass/stage5/approvals.json`, which the same run writes. Stage 5 is example-only: it took the 409 verb entries that still had no example after Stage 4 through the pass again, and the skeptic read every proposal, corpus and quotation picks included.

## Glosses (Stage 5c)

25 gloss changes proposed in Stage 5c, for verbs whose example kept failing because the gloss misstated the verb; 23 stand. Each was proposed with CNRTL dictionary evidence (TLFi, Académie, Littré) and judged by the skeptic. A verb's example below may fit only its proposed gloss, so accept or reject the two together.

#### liquider (rank 1486)
- **Gloss:** liquidate, settle a debt, kill → **liquidate, settle (a debt or a matter), sell off, kill (slang)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, high confidence. Académie 9e: '2. COMMERCE. Vendre à bas prix des marchandises que l'on souhaite écouler rapidement. Liquider son stock'; TLFi A.3 'Vendre, écouler des marchandises à bas prix'. The shipped gloss lacks 'sell off'; Wiktionnaire 1 'Régler définitivement une affaire en cours' is covered by 'matter'.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. The shipped gloss is missing a main sense: Académie 9e gives '2. COMMERCE. Vendre à bas prix des marchandises… Liquider son stock' and TLFi A.3 gives 'Vendre, écouler des marchandises à bas prix'. The proposal adds 'sell off', covers Wiktionnaire 1 'Régler définitivement une affaire en cours' with 'matter' rather than the false friend 'affair', and labels the killing sense, which Wiktionnaire marks 'Argot' and TLFi 'Au fig., fam.'. It is exactly the fix the earlier skeptic asked for.

#### baiser (rank 1863)
- **Gloss:** copulate with → **kiss (literary), copulate with (vulgar)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: "Effleurer, toucher de ses lèvres quelque partie d'une personne (surtout la main, la joue) ou quelque objet"; Académie 9e sense 1: "Poser, appliquer ses lèvres sur quelqu'un ou quelque chose en signe d'affection, de respect", with the sexual sense only at 2 (Populaire); Littré leads with "Appliquer sa bouche sur le visage, la main ou un objet". Wiktionnaire notes the kiss sense survives only in set uses (baiser la main, l'anneau). The shipped gloss lacks the kiss sense, which every dictionary leads with.
- **Skeptic:** partly, error. The shipped gloss really does omit the sense every dictionary leads with: TLFi "toucher de ses lèvres quelque partie d'une personne ... ou quelque objet", Académie 9e sense 1 "appliquer ses lèvres sur quelqu'un ou quelque chose" (sexual sense only at 2, "Populaire"), and Littré "Appliquer sa bouche sur le visage, la main ou un objet". So adding "kiss (literary)" is right, but house style puts the commonest sense first, and the Wiktionnaire usage note the checker itself cites says the opposite of its ordering: "À cause de la prééminence du sens obscène, ce verbe n'est pratiquement plus utilisé actuellement dans cette acception". The right gloss is "copulate with (vulgar), kiss (literary)".

#### arriérer (rank 2680)
- **Gloss:** tarry, be late → **put off (a payment), tarry, be late (dated)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, medium confidence. Académie 9e: 'Vieilli. 1. Différer... Arriérer un paiement'; TLFi 'Vx. Retarder'. The transitive sense is the app's non-pronominal use; 'tarry, be late' is the pronominal s'arriérer.
- **Skeptic:** partly, hedge. Adding the transitive sense is right: Académie 9e has 'Vieilli. 1. Différer… Arriérer un paiement' and TLFi 'Vx. Retarder', which is the non-pronominal verb the app conjugates. But keeping 'tarry' (linger) misrenders s'arriérer, which Académie glosses 'se mettre en retard, dans ses paiements, dans son travail', and '(dated)' hangs on the last sense only. Better: 'put off (a payment), fall behind (dated)'.

#### charpenter (rank 2818)
- **Gloss:** tailor, tailor make → **hew timber, structure (a speech or play)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: 'Tailler, équarrir des pièces de bois'; Wiktionnaire/Académie: figuratively 'Bien façonner, structurer... Charpenter habilement son discours'. 'Tailor' (the English Wiktionary reading) is a different verb.
- **Skeptic:** partly, error. 'tailor, tailor make' is wrong: TLFi gives 'Tailler, équarrir des pièces de bois' and 'Construire en établissant un plan solide. Charpenter un discours'. But Académie 9e marks the timber sense '(rare)' and says the verb is 'Employé surtout au participe passé… Figuré. Une plaidoirie bien charpentée', so the order should be 'structure (a speech or play), hew timber'.

#### baster (rank 3906)
- **Gloss:** suffice, give satisfaction → **give in (Switzerland), suffice, give satisfaction (dated)**
- **Checker:** `order`, medium confidence. TLFi: 'A. Vx. Suffire; donner satisfaction (Ac. 1798-1878)... B. Région. Céder'; Wiktionnaire: 'Vieilli. Suffire' and '(Suisse) Céder à une pression'. The live sense is the Swiss one; the shipped gloss has only the dated one.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. The shipped gloss gives only the dead sense: TLFi has 'A. Vx. Suffire' with the note 'inus. dans Quillet 1965'. The live use is 'B. Région. Céder' (Molliex 1972; Wiktionnaire '(Suisse) Céder à une pression', with Bouvier 1985). Leading with 'give in (Switzerland)' and labeling the old sense dated is correct.

#### harper (rank 4336)
- **Gloss:** grasp forcefully → **lift a hind leg high (of a horse), grasp forcefully (dated)**
- **Checker:** `order`, high confidence. Académie 9e: 'HARPER verbe intransitif. ÉQUITATION. En parlant d'un cheval. Lever une des jambes de derrière plus haut que l'autre' listed first; 'HARPER verbe transitif. Vieilli. Prendre et serrer fortement avec les mains.' The grasping sense is dated and needs the label.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. The shipped gloss presents the grasping sense as current, but Académie 9e marks it 'Vieilli. Prendre et serrer fortement avec les mains', TLFi marks it 'Vieilli, pop.' and Wiktionnaire tags it 'dated'. Académie 9e lists the unlabeled 'ÉQUITATION En parlant d'un cheval. Lever une des jambes de derrière plus haut que l'autre' first, so the proposed order and label are correct, and they match the earlier skeptic's fix.

#### insoler (rank 4597)
- **Gloss:** sunbathe → **sunbathe (dated), expose to light (technical)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, medium confidence. Littré: 'Terme didactique. Exposer au soleil... S'insoler, verbe réfléchi: S'exposer aux rayons du soleil'; TLFi: 'verbe transitif. Domaine techn. Exposer au soleil ou à une lumière artificielle', PHOT. et HÉLIOGRAVURE; Wiktionnaire 2 circuit-board sense. The reflexive sunbathing sense is attested only in Littré (didactic), and the live use is technical.
- **Skeptic:** refuted, hedge. The '(dated)' label has no source: Littré calls the verb 'Terme didactique' and gives 's'insoler… S'exposer aux rayons du soleil' with no dated or vieilli tag. The checker says 'the live use is technical' yet still leads with the sense it calls dated. The app ships the verb as pronominal (passé composé 'suis insolé'), so adding TLFi's transitive 'Exposer au soleil ou à une lumière artificielle' would gloss a construction the app does not conjugate. The flag would have to be settled first, and no flag change is proposed.

#### laitonner (rank 4844)
- **Gloss:** plate with brass → **trim with brass wire, plate with brass (technical)**
- **Checker:** `order`, medium confidence. Académie 9e: TECHNIQUE '1. Garnir de fils de laiton... 2. Recouvrir par des procédés électrolytiques d'une mince couche de laiton'. Both senses are technical; the shipped gloss has only the second.
- **Skeptic:** partly, hedge. The missing sense is real: Académie 9e gives '1. Garnir de fils de laiton. Laitonner un abat-jour' before '2. Recouvrir… d'une mince couche de laiton'. But 'trim' suggests decoration, while the wire stiffens hat forms and lampshades (TLFi 'Laitonner une forme de chapeau'). Also, '(technical)' tags only the second sense though Académie marks both TECHNIQUE. Ship 'fit with brass wire, plate with brass'.

#### pacquer (rank 5118)
- **Gloss:** pack → **pack salted fish in barrels (fishing)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: 'Emballer, disposer en couches (le poisson salé) dans un baril'; Académie 9e: 'Disposer le poisson salé par couches, dans un baril... Pacquer des morues'; Littré: 'Terme de pêche'.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. Bare 'pack' would send a learner to pacquer for any packing. TLFi gives 'Emballer, disposer en couches (le poisson salé) dans un baril', Académie 9e 'Disposer le poisson salé par couches, dans un baril… Pacquer des morues', and Littré 'Terme de pêche'.

#### amurer (rank 5214)
- **Gloss:** tack → **haul taut the tack of a sail (nautical)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, medium confidence. Académie 9e: 'MARINE. Tendre, raidir, étarquer plus ou moins l'amure d'une voile'; TLFi: 'Fixer l'amure d'une voile pour l'orienter selon le vent'. English 'tack' means changing course, a different maneuver.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. English 'tack' means changing course through the wind, but Académie 9e defines amurer as 'Tendre, raidir, étarquer plus ou moins l'amure d'une voile' and TLFi as 'Fixer l'amure d'une voile pour l'orienter selon le vent'. The proposal names that operation, with a field label.

#### crouter (rank 5299)
- **Gloss:** eat, form a crust → **eat (slang), form a crust (dated)**
- **Checker:** `style`, medium confidence. TLFi croûter: 'A. Arg. Manger... B. Vx et rare. Couvrir d'une croûte, former une croûte'; Académie 9e: 'Populaire. Manger'. Senses are right, register labels missing.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. The senses are right but unlabeled. TLFi marks 'A. Arg. Manger' and 'B. Vx et rare. Couvrir d'une croûte, former une croûte', Académie 9e has 'Populaire. Manger', and Wiktionnaire 'Familier'. Register labels in parentheses are house style.

#### rapiner (rank 5334)
- **Gloss:** pillage, plunder → **pilfer, embezzle, plunder (dated)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. Gloss attempt upheld earlier. Académie 9e: 'Vieilli. S'emparer du bien d'autrui par rapine, particulièrement en abusant de la fonction qu'on exerce'; TLFi B.1 'se livrer à de petits vols'; Wiktionnaire 'Prendre injustement, voler'.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. 'pillage, plunder' misstates the main sense. Académie 9e gives 'Vieilli. S'emparer du bien d'autrui par rapine, particulièrement en abusant de la fonction qu'on exerce', TLFi B.1 gives 'sans idée explicite de violence… se livrer à de petits vols. Synon. chaparder', and Wiktionnaire gives 'Prendre injustement, voler… en abusant des fonctions'. 'pilfer, embezzle' leads with those senses, and 'plunder (dated)' keeps the older violent sense with a register label that Académie 9e's 'Vieilli' supports.

#### bretter (rank 5336)
- **Gloss:** pick a fight, pick a quarrel → **dress stone with a toothed chisel, dawdle (Quebec), pick a quarrel (obsolete)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, high confidence. TLFi BRETTER2: 'ARCHIT. Dresser le parement d'une pierre à l'aide d'un outil denté'; Académie 8e 'Pratiquer des dentelures, en taillant de la pierre'; Wiktionnaire '(Québec) Prendre son temps inutilement' and 'Vieilli. Obsolète. Faire le bretteur, chercher noise'. The shipped gloss gives only the obsolete sense. Reviewer partly upheld this proposal.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. The shipped gloss gives only the sense Wiktionnaire tags 'Vieilli. Obsolète. Faire le bretteur, chercher noise', which TLFi says Lar. 20e marks 'vieux'. The proposal leads with TLFi BRETTER2 'ARCHIT. Dresser le parement d'une pierre à l'aide d'un outil denté', which TLFi says is 'Attesté dans la plupart des dict.', adds Wiktionnaire's '(Québec) Prendre son temps inutilement' and labels the obsolete sense. It is the earlier skeptic's recommended wording.

#### margoter (rank 5384)
- **Gloss:** make a mating call → **call (of a quail)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: 'MARGOTER verbe intransitif. [En parlant de la caille] Pousser son cri'; Wiktionnaire: 'zoologie → voir margotter'. 'Mating call' is not in the evidence.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. TLFi gives 'MARGOTER verbe intransitif. [En parlant de la caille] Pousser son cri', a quail-specific call. The shipped 'make a mating call' is not in the evidence and hides the restriction to quail. 'call (of a quail)' uses the parenthesis to fix the sense, as house style allows.

#### déparler (rank 5608)
- **Gloss:** speak nastily → **talk nonsense, speak ill (regional), stop talking (dated)**
- **Checker:** `order`, medium confidence. TLFi: 'A. Vieilli. S'arrêter de parler... B. Région. Parler inconsidérément; dire n'importe quoi'; Wiktionnaire: '(Régionalisme) Parler en mal... médire'. The commonest current use is 'talk nonsense', which the gloss lacks.
- **Skeptic:** partly, hedge. Leading with 'talk nonsense' is right; Wiktionnaire's quotations (Giono, Pagnol, Mauriac, Vargas) all show it. But TLFi labels that sense 'B. Région. Parler inconsidérément' and Wiktionnaire '(Régionalisme)', and the proposal marks only 'speak ill' as regional. Both senses need the regional label.

#### panneauter (rank 5753)
- **Gloss:** put in a cold frame → **set nets for small game (hunting), cover with panels**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: 'A. CHASSE. Chasser avec des panneaux. Panneauter des lapins... B. HORTIC. Couvrir de panneaux'; Académie 8e: 'Tendre des panneaux pour prendre du menu gibier'. 'Cold frame' is only the gardening sub-sense.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. Hunting is every dictionary's first sense: TLFi 'A. CHASSE. Chasser avec des panneaux. Panneauter des lapins', Académie 9e '1. CHASSE', and Académie 8e's only sense 'Tendre des panneaux pour prendre du menu gibier'. The shipped 'put in a cold frame' misstates even the gardening sense ('Couvrir de panneaux. Panneauter une couche').

#### écuisser (rank 5866)
- **Gloss:** split a tree trunk → **splinter a tree trunk (forestry)**
- **Checker:** `style`, medium confidence. Littré: 'Faire éclater le tronc d'un arbre en l'abattant'; TLFi: 'Faire éclater le tronc de (un arbre)'. 'Éclater' is to burst or splinter, not to split cleanly; a forestry term.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. Littré gives 'Faire éclater le tronc d'un arbre en l'abattant' and TLFi 'Faire éclater le tronc'. Éclater is to burst or splinter, the damage of a bad felling, while 'split' suggests deliberate cleaving (fendre), which is the mistake the prior example made.

#### jabler (rank 5895)
- **Gloss:** create a lip on the end of a barrel → **cut a groove in barrel staves (cooperage)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. Académie 9e: 'TONNELLERIE. Creuser un jable dans une douve'; Littré: 'Faire le jable des douves'. A jable is the groove in a stave, not a lip.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. Académie 9e defines jabler as 'TONNELLERIE. Creuser un jable dans une douve', TLFi as 'Creuser le jable (dans les douves d'un tonneau)', and Littré as 'Faire le jable des douves'. A jable is a groove cut into the staves, not a lip.

#### cocoter (rank 5934)
- **Gloss:** yap, jabber, blather → **stink (slang), yap, jabber, blather**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, medium confidence. TLFi COCOT(T)ER2: 'Arg. Sentir mauvais, puer'; Wiktionnaire: 'Autre orthographe de cocotter' with 'Puer' (familiar). 'Bavarder inlassablement' is TLFi's rare sense A; 'stink' is missing.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. TLFi labels the chattering sense 'Rare' and gives 'COCOT(T)ER2… Arg. Sentir mauvais, puer'. Both Wiktionnaire quotations for cocoter (Rictus, Perret) and the Barbusse concordance use the stink sense, so it is the main sense and is missing.

#### cocotter (rank 5935)
- **Gloss:** yap, jabber, blather → **stink (slang), yap, jabber, blather**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, medium confidence. Wiktionnaire: '1. Familier. Puer. 2. Aménager quelque chose à son goût'; TLFi: 'Arg. Sentir mauvais, puer'. The shipped gloss gives only the rare chattering sense (TLFi 'Rare... Bavarder inlassablement').
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. Wiktionnaire's cocotter senses are '1. Familier. Puer. 2. Aménager quelque chose à son goût'; the shipped 'yap' corresponds only to TLFi's 'Rare… Bavarder inlassablement'. Adding 'stink (slang)' first is correct.

#### terser (rank 6005)
- **Gloss:** plow a third time → **give a vineyard its third plowing (viticulture)**
- **Checker:** `style`, medium confidence. Académie 8e and Littré: 'TERCER ou TERSER. Donner un troisième labour, une troisième façon à la vigne'; Wiktionnaire tercer: '(Viticulture)'. Meaning is right; register and object are missing.
- **Skeptic:** partly, nitpick. The change is worth making: Académie 8e and Littré give 'Donner un troisième labour, une troisième façon à la vigne', and the prior sentence ('terser leurs champs') shows the bare gloss misleads. But '(viticulture)' after 'vineyard' is a redundant field label, which house style forbids. Ship 'plow (a vineyard) a third time'.

#### énouer (rank 6011)
- **Gloss:** remove the knots from, unknot → **remove knots and debris from cloth (textiles)**
- **Checker:** `style`, medium confidence. Académie 9e: 'TEXTILE. Débarrasser un tissu de ses nœuds et des corps étrangers visibles à sa surface'; Littré: 'Éplucher le drap'. The object is cloth; 'unknot' misleads.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. 'unknot' suggests untying knots, but Académie 9e gives 'TEXTILE. Débarrasser un tissu de ses nœuds et des corps étrangers visibles à sa surface. Énouer un drap de laine' and Littré 'Éplucher le drap'. The proposal names the cloth object and the debris.

#### surcomprimer (rank 6043)
- **Gloss:** supercharge → **overcompress, raise the compression ratio of (an engine)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. TLFi: 'TECHNOL. Porter le taux de compression des gaz (dans un moteur à explosion) au-delà des capacités normales... Moteur surcomprimé'; also figurative 'comprimer d'une manière extrême'. 'Supercharge' (forced induction) is a different technique.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. TLFi defines surcomprimer as 'Porter le taux de compression des gaz (dans un moteur à explosion) au-delà des capacités normales… Moteur surcomprimé'. That is high compression, not supercharging (forced induction), so the shipped gloss is wrong and the proposal names the right sense.

#### rentrayer (rank 6124)
- **Gloss:** mend a tear invisibly, reweave → **sew up buffalo hides, mend cloth invisibly (textiles)**
- **Checker:** `missing_primary_sense`, low confidence. Littré and Wiktionnaire: 'Recoudre les peaux de buffles' is the only defined sense; Académie 9e: 'TEXTILE. Synonyme de Rentraire' (not defined in the shard). The shipped gloss gives only the invisible-mending sense, which rests on knowledge of rentraire, not on the shard.
- **Skeptic:** refuted, hedge. Nothing in the shard shows the current gloss is wrong. Académie 9e, the most recent source, files the verb under 'TEXTILE. Synonyme de Rentraire', and the proposal keeps the cloth sense too. It puts Littré's 19th-century 'Recoudre les peaux de buffles' first with no evidence that this is the commonest sense, on the checker's own 'low' confidence. The '(textiles)' parenthesis adds nothing to 'mend cloth'.

#### rengrener (rank 6311)
- **Gloss:** refill with grain → **re-mesh gear teeth, restrike a coin or medal (minting)**
- **Checker:** `wrong_sense`, high confidence. Académie 9e: 'MÉCANIQUE. Engager de nouveau les crans, les dents d'un mécanisme... MONNAIES. Remettre sous le balancier... les pièces, les médailles dont l'empreinte est mal venue'. Nothing about refilling with grain.
- **Skeptic:** upheld, error. None of the dictionaries in the shard gives a grain sense. Académie 9e has '1. MÉCANIQUE Engager de nouveau les crans, les dents d'un mécanisme… Rengrener les pignons. 2. MONNAIES. Remettre sous le balancier… les médailles', and Académie 8e and Wiktionnaire give the minting sense. The proposal follows that order.


## Examples standing

409 upheld or partly, in rank order. A `partly` verdict says what would fix the item; type the corrected French and English as its value, or reject it.

#### sortir (obtain) (rank 78)
- **Gloss:** obtain
- **French:** La décision sortissait son plein effet dès le lendemain de la signature.
- **English:** The decision took full effect the day after it was signed.
- **Source:** Claude (Sonnet 5.5) · token sortissait
- **Before:** flawed example removed in Stage 4
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. The prior skeptic asked for exactly this: a 'sortir son (plein) effet' sentence in an -iss- form. 'sortissait' matches the app's imparfait.thirdSingular row, and the sense is en Wiktionary's 'to have or take' (its own example: 'sortissant son plein et entier effet' = 'taking its full & entire effect') and Wiktionnaire's 'Obtenir, avoir'. No candidate uses the -iss- homograph, so authoring was needed, and 'took full effect' is a faithful translation.
- *Note:* No candidate uses the legal -iss- homograph (sortir son effet); every candidate is ordinary sortir, so a sentence was authored.

#### remettre (rank 159)
- **Gloss:** put back, replace
- **French:** Les couvre-caméra ne devraient être retirés que lors de l’utilisation volontaire de la caméra et être ensuite immédiatement remis.
- **English:** The camera covers should be removed only when the camera is deliberately being used, and then put back immediately afterward.
- **Source:** ch-ncsc-protection-appareils.txt:38 · provenance `verbatim` · token remis
- **Before:** flawed example removed in Stage 4
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. Verbatim corpus line (ch-ncsc-protection-appareils.txt:38). 'être ensuite immédiatement remis' is a verbal passive in the glossed sense 'put back' (Wiktionnaire 'Mettre une chose à l’endroit où elle était auparavant'), and the translation is faithful. It avoids the prior failure, which used the reflexive 's’en remettre à'.

#### élire (rank 344)
- **Gloss:** elect
- **French:** En 1793, par décret daté du 8 août, la Convention supprime toutes les académies royales (« royaume des lettrés, titrés, mitrés » selon la formule de Chamfort), y compris l'Académie française, et confirme l'interdiction d'élire de nouveaux membres pour remplacer ceux décédés.
- **English:** In 1793, by a decree dated August 8, the Convention abolished all the royal academies ("kingdom of the lettered, titled, and mitered," in Chamfort's phrase), including the Académie française, and confirmed the ban on electing new members to replace those who had died.
- **Source:** wp-academie-francaise.txt:27 · provenance `verbatim` · token élire
- **Before:** flawed example removed in Stage 4
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. Verbatim corpus line (wp-academie-francaise.txt:27). 'l'interdiction d'élire de nouveaux membres' is the glossed 'elect' sense ('Nommer … par la voie des suffrages'), not the 'élire domicile' idiom that sank the prior example. The translation is faithful.

#### réjouir (rank 753)
- **Gloss:** please, delight
- **French:** Tout venait de s'écrouler en elle et autour d'elle, et rien n'avait plus le pouvoir de toucher son cœur, de le réjouir ou de l'attrister.
- **English:** Everything had just collapsed within her and around her, and nothing had any more power to touch her heart, to delight it or to sadden it.
- **Source:** wiktionnaire: Isabelle Eberhardt, Yasmina, 1902, d. 1904 · provenance `verbatim` · token réjouir
- **Before:** flawed example removed in Stage 4
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. Verbatim Wiktionnaire quotation by Isabelle Eberhardt, death_year 1904 (before 1931), so it is public domain. 'toucher son cœur, de le réjouir' is transitive 'Donner de la joie', i.e. the glossed 'please, delight', not the pronominal 'se réjouir' that failed before. 'to delight it or to sadden it' is faithful.
- *Note:* Gloss lacks the pronominal se réjouir (rejoice), which is the commonest use; consider adding it in a later pass.

#### plaindre (rank 755)
- **Gloss:** pity
- **French:** Vraiment, par expérience, je plains le jeune prêtre que l’on envoie, pour ses débuts, évangéliser la Beauce.

- **Gloss:** copulate with
- **French:** je crois en l’Être suprême, à un Créateur, quel qu’il soit, peu m’importe, qui nous a placés ici-bas pour y remplir nos devoirs de citoyen et de père de famille; mais je n’ai pas besoin d’aller, dans une église, baiser des plats d’argent, et engraisser de ma poche un tas de farceurs qui se nourrissent mieux que nous!
- **English:** I believe in the Supreme Being, in a Creator, whoever he may be, I don’t much care, who placed us here below to fulfill our duties as citizens and fathers of families; but I have no need to go into a church to kiss silver plates and fatten, out of my own pocket, a bunch of jokers who eat better than we do!
- **Source:** flaubert-madame-bovary-1857.txt:2909 · provenance `verbatim` · token baiser
- **Before:** Stage 5 example replaced at Josh's request
- **Skeptic:** upheld, hedge. This is a tier corpus pick (Madame Bovary, line 2909) with pick_status "verbatim". The French matches the candidate exactly, and it is a corpus line, so no quotation death_year applies. The infinitive "baiser des plats d'argent" is verbal and in the devotional kiss sense that Académie 8e lists ("Baiser la croix, des reliques ... la patène"). It is literary and contains nothing sexual or vulgar, as prior.hint requires, and it repeats neither earlier attempt. The translation is faithful, including "peu m'importe" as "I don't much care" and "farceurs" as "jokers". It does not fit gloss_current "copulate with", so it can ship only with a gloss that includes "kiss (literary)".
- *Note:* Per Josh's PG request, the example uses the kiss sense (kissing church plates, a literary and formal use). It is the first qualifying candidate: verbal, kiss sense, complete sentence. The earlier candidates are the noun un baiser.
- *Note:* The Dumas quotation "Nous baisâmes les livres saints..." (death_year 1870, token baisâmes) is a shorter, cleaner fallback if the Flaubert sentence is judged too long for a learner.

#### bémoliser (rank 5649)

#### haricoter (rank 6023)
- **Gloss:** take small risks in gambling or business

The translation is wrong, though: 'wheeled and dealed' suggests shrewd, ambitious scheming, the opposite of 'mesquinement', and 'in small-time ventures' adds words the French does not have. Ship the French with something like 'For years, old Lucas made petty deals without ever making a fortune.'

#### rentrayer (rank 6124)

Combler sur l'envers les relais d'une tapisserie ou réparer à l'aiguille les parties usées'. TLFi B gives 'refaire la trame ou la chaîne d'une tapisserie endommagée'. So a tapestry restorer reweaving the holes in a hanging thread by thread fits the shipped 'reweave', and the dictionaries show gaps ('relais') as the object. The form 'rentrayait' matches the app's imparfait, no dictionary example is copied, and the translation is faithful.

The 'dated' tag describes the verb as a whole, which the gloss already accepts.

## Verbs that stay without an example

Each verb below stays without an example: the skeptic refuted its Stage 5 proposal. The reason says what was wrong.

| Rank | Verb | Gloss | Proposed | Source | Skeptic’s reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Gloss and flag remarks for a later pass

Stage 5 checkers were told to leave glosses and flags alone and to note anything wrong. 139 verbs carry notes; they are not decisions.

- **sortir (obtain)** (78): No candidate uses the legal -iss- homograph (sortir son effet); every candidate is ordinary sortir, so a sentence was authored.
- **réjouir** (753): Gloss lacks the pronominal se réjouir (rejoice), which is the commonest use; consider adding it in a later pass.
- **plaindre** (755): Gloss lacks se plaindre (complain), a very common use; consider adding in a later pass.
- **trancher** (799): Candidates only show the 'contrast' sense, 'trancher du grand seigneur', or truncated or verse lines; none fits the gloss. Gloss omits 'contrast, stand out'.
- **gazer** (975): Usage example copied unchanged from the editors' candidate (lowercase initial and no space before the question mark are the source's own typography).
- **dialoguer** (1137): Every candidate is the noun dialogue; the only verbal example is a copyrighted English-Wiktionary quotation.
- **vanter** (1188): Gloss lacks se vanter (boast, brag), a common use.
- **décoller** (1196): The tier line about airplanes taking off carries a stray heading word ('Impacts') at its start, so it was not used.
- **homologuer** (1308): The tier line with 'a homologué' opens with a stray heading ('Constatation forestière'), so it was not used.
- **terrer** (1365): Gloss covers only the transitive 'spread with soil'; the pronominal se terrer (hide, go to ground) is the common use and is missing. All candidates are the noun terre or the slang 'guillotiner'.
- **superviser** (1399): Tier line 311 (ch-ge-rapport-gestion) is truncated mid-word ("trans-"), so it was skipped.
- **abîmer** (1425): Prior pick cited line 7548; the sentence is at line 717 (cited correctly here).
- **marrer** (1565): Candidates were the noun marrons plus an Olivier de Serres line with the hoeing sense, which the gloss does not cover; the only usable English-Wiktionary quotation is under copyright (Zaz), so a sentence was authored.
- **ébranler** (1620): Tier line 7 (ch-fed-suisse-numerique) is cut off mid-sentence, so it was skipped.
- **alourdir** (1667): Zola line 11167 (s'alourdir, 'become heavy') was passed over because the gloss 'weigh down, get worse' fits the transitive sense better. The gloss itself (class none_verbatim) may merit a later look: Wiktionnaire's lead senses are 'Rendre lourd' and pronominal 'Devenir lourd'.
- **cadrer** (1682): Only corpus candidate for the verb (Proust, line 13756) is a subordinate clause with no main clause; the rest are the noun cadre. Authored instead.
- **déchiffrer** (1702): Proust line 10735 skipped: its quotation mark is left open (editorial truncation).
- **salir** (1716): Bovary line 7401 begins lowercase mid-sentence ("car il était là..."), so it was skipped.
- **débiter** (1734): Earlier candidates (Bovary, Molière) use the 'recite, reel off' sense, which the shipped gloss ('sell continuously, produce') does not list, so they were skipped.
- **médicaliser** (1787): Authored to the 'donner un caractère médical' sense, which matches English 'medicalize'; the earlier refuted sentence used the 'equip' sense.
- **acharner** (1792): English Wiktionary usage example (usage: true), chosen because the corpus and quotation candidates all use the pronominal s'acharner, which the gloss 'incite, sic' does not cover.
- **monder** (1809): Sentence taken whole from the wiktionnaire candidate (André Theuriet, d. 1907). The earlier pick trimmed it. The Huysmans quotation was skipped because 'mondée' there is an adjectival participle.
- **mouvementer** (1831): Authored to Wiktionnaire's 'Faire bouger' sense, which the shipped gloss 'set something in motion' covers. English Wiktionary's only sense is the banking 'activate/use an account', which the gloss does not cover; that mismatch may merit a later gloss look.
- **baiser** (1863): Per Josh's PG request, the example uses the kiss sense (kissing church plates, a literary and formal use). It is the first qualifying candidate: verbal, kiss sense, complete sentence. The earlier candidates are the noun un baiser. · The Dumas quotation "Nous baisâmes les livres saints..." (death_year 1870, token baisâmes) is a shorter, cleaner fallback if the Flaubert sentence is judged too long for a learner.
- **bouler** (1896): Used intransitively per Wiktionnaire 'Enfler sa gorge, en parlant du pigeon'. Both quotation candidates use the 'knock down / kill' sense, which the gloss does not cover; corpus candidates are the noun boule.
- **exclamer** (1903): Every corpus candidate is a fragment (lowercase start, unclosed quote, or bare tag clause).
- **historier** (2019): Gloss 'spin a yarn' rests on Wiktionnaire 'Décrire, raconter'; a later pass may want to check it reads as 'recount'.
- **épingler** (2033): Every candidate is the noun épingle.
- **graduer** (2097): Gloss 'calibrate' is closer to étalonner; 'mark with degrees' is the faithful sense.
- **clicher** (2150): The only tier candidate is the noun cliché; the en-wiktionary quotation is under copyright.
- **limoger** (2283): Proust (d. 1922) quotation with nested guillemets; if a cleaner sentence is preferred, author one around 'a limogé le directeur'.
- **graviter** (2286): Every tier candidate is the noun gravité.
- **féconder** (2375): Figurative 'fertilize' use; the Zola quotation 'fécondant encore leurs épouses' would illustrate the 'impregnate' sense if a literal one is wanted.
- **entrouvrir** (2484): Pronominal use in this quotation; the en-wiktionary entry tags the sense pronominal, so the re flag audit may be worth a later look.
- **cloisonner** (2512): Tier candidates use cloisonné as an adjective or are truncated.
- **oxyder** (2522): Transitive use, avoiding the earlier ungrammatical 'Le fer oxyde'. Candidates are all oxydant/oxydes.
- **magasiner** (2534): Quebec usage in every sense tag; a later pass might consider '(Quebec)' in the gloss. The wiktionnaire quotation candidate is truncated at its start and carries a bogus death_year.
- **diligenter** (2564): Candidates are a Latin line and the adjective diligente. Modern diligenter une enquête means 'order/launch', which the gloss 'expedite' does not cover.
- **espionner** (2597): Tier candidates skipped: dialogue fragment with insult, editorial fragment, mid-sentence fragment, or pronouns with no antecedent.
- **schématiser** (2598): Both corpus candidates are lead-ins ending in a colon or fragments.
- **surtaxer** (2627): Gloss lint: same gloss as surimposer; later pass may want to differentiate.
- **arriérer** (2680): Sue quotation candidate is truncated with '...', so rejected. · Dictionaries mark the 'be late' senses pronominal (s'arriérer) while the app conjugates the verb non-pronominally; flag re may merit a later look.
- **abstraire** (2754): audits.flags: dg 25 has no defective marking in either Wiktionary edition; left for a later pass to review.
- **tiercer** (2791): Gloss lint: no overlap with English Wiktionary (which only says 'alternative spelling of tercer'); gloss is a translation of the French first sense, looks faithful.
- **acculer** (2810): Scene is an unwanted advance; acceptable but a later pass may prefer an authored neutral sentence.
- **bicher** (2831): All corpus candidates are the noun 'biche' (doe) or the name Biche.
- **ramifier** (2854): audits.flags: re=true (pronominal-only) agrees with Wiktionnaire, whose senses all define 'Se partager'; the app flag looks right.
- **transir** (2967): Racine (d. 1699), public domain; four-line verse with embedded newlines, taken verbatim as the skeptic directed. Gloss 'chill to the bone' fits the figurative shiver only loosely.
- **débaucher** (2993): Gloss leads with 'make redundant, lay off', whereas English Wiktionary leads with 'debauch', and 'lure away / headhunt' is the commoner current sense; later pass may reconsider order.
- **tonner** (3037): Figurative 'thundered' (spoke with vehemence); old verse typography kept verbatim.
- **magner** (3061): Infinitive used so the token matches an app form; the earlier imperative 'magne-toi' failed the conjugation check.
- **pourfendre** (3077): The only candidate is a line of verse split across lines (Rollinat), not a clean sentence, so one was authored.
- **délester** (3105): Candidates illustrate decongesting traffic, which the shipped gloss does not cover; the gloss may lack the figurative senses (decongest, relieve, rob).
- **défausser** (3184): Wiktionnaire's pronominal senses (discard a card, shed responsibility) are the commoner modern uses, yet the gloss is only 'straighten' and re=false; worth a later look.
- **carbonater** (3207): Candidates are the noun 'carbonate' only.
- **mâtiner** (3318): Candidates are the noun 'mâtine' (mongrel/hussy).
- **kilométrer** (3330): Infinitive used to avoid the earlier irregular-form problem (é to è stem change).
- **croûter** (3369): Candidates are the noun 'croûte'.
- **tuber** (3382): Candidates are the noun 'tube'.
- **quémander** (3519): Pick copied verbatim, including the lowercase start of the corpus line.
- **trompeter** (3533): The conjugation check earlier found no app form matching 'trompette' for model 1-4; possible engine bug worth checking. Infinitive used here.
- **bagarrer** (3563): The fighting sense is mainly pronominal (se bagarrer) while re=false; worth a later look.
- **pointiller** (3574): The Académie candidate opens with an unbalanced guillemet, so it was not taken.
- **peinturer** (3666): Candidate list is all the noun peinture; English-Wiktionary usage example is a childish 'paint badly' sense, so a sentence was authored.
- **interpénétrer** (3670): Wiktionnaire's sense is reciprocal and the natural form is s’interpénétrer; flag re is false in the app, which a later pass may want to revisit.
- **poiler** (3679): Standard spelling is se poiler (poiler); informal register.
- **orbiter** (3690): Candidates are all the noun orbite(s).
- **fesser** (3714): Tier Moliere sentence is a very long verse sentence and the Anatole France quotation is crude; the editors' usage example is clean.
- **vociférer** (3716): Gustave Aimard died 1883. The Flaubert tier candidate is a truncated fragment.
- **intriquer** (3748): Rare, literary or scientific verb; sentence kept abstract on purpose.
- **engoncer** (3778): Candidates use the participle in a different configuration (neck sunk into shoulders) or of snow; authored sentence matches the clothing sense in the gloss.
- **castagner** (3788): Sentence uses the intransitive/pronominal brawl sense the gloss covers, not transitive 'beat up'; a later pass may want to add that sense to the gloss.
- **glisser-déposer** (3812): Infinitive used to avoid the invariant-first-element conjugation issue.
- **alcooliser** (3825): Gloss 'alcoholize' is thin; the pronominal s’alcooliser (drink to intoxication) is also common, so a later pass could look at the gloss.
- **égrainer** (3877): Variant of égrener; stripping kernels from ears of corn is a standard égrener use.
- **baster** (3906): The only candidate is a Molière footnote on Italian 'basta', not a verb.

- **potasser** (3994): Candidates are all the noun potasse.
- **étrenner** (3999): Tier candidates are the noun étrennes; the Flaubert quotation carries a […] and the Vallès one is dialogue.
- **imperméabiliser** (4002): The tier candidate carries an editorial '(→ Sols)' and the earlier pick dropped it, so it is not usable verbatim.

- **détourer** (4039): Gloss 'silhouette' is weak for the cut-out-an-image sense; a later pass might prefer 'cut out (an image)'.
- **roupiller** (4042): Eugène Sue died 1857.
- **corseter** (4083): Imperfect chosen to avoid the corsète stem change.
- **ébrécher** (4097): Zola candidate uses ébréché as an adjective.

- **platiner** (4326): Candidates were all the noun platine/Platina. Wiktionnaire also lists a hair-coloring sense not covered by the gloss 'platinize'; consider adding it in a later pass.
- **tripatouiller** (4471): Only candidate was the groping usage example, which the gloss 'tamper with' does not cover; authored a tampering sentence instead.
- **relaver** (4591): The Gotthelf quotation is a 1999 translation from German, rejected earlier; not reused.
- **insoler** (4597): Littré: 'S'insoler, verbe réfléchi. S'exposer aux rayons du soleil' (Terme didactique), so the pronominal use is attested, in a learned register. TLFi and Wiktionnaire give only the transitive technical sense (including photo/PCB exposure); flag re may deserve a later look.
- **dépiler** (4614): Uses the tanning sense; cosmetic hair removal is épiler in modern French.
- **invertir** (4625): Corpus candidates use inverti only as an adjective in sucre inverti, or in a long fragment; rejected.
- **caleter** (4626): Infinitive used because the app's forms (caleté) differ from the common spelling calter. Slang register; the dg 5 flag has no support in either edition, and the gloss lacks a (slang) label.
- **admonester** (4672): Second sentence of the Moselly quotation, lifted unchanged.
- **engrener** (4690): The en-Wiktionary usage examples are phrase fragments, and the Gautier quotation uses the gear sense in a passive adjectival way, so a sentence was authored for the fill-with-grain sense.
- **encroûter** (4824): Second sentence of the Gautier quotation, lifted unchanged.
- **fuseler** (4827): Infinitive used to avoid the fusèle/fuselle spelling question; model 1-3A should give fuselle.
- **gendarmer** (4840): Gloss 'militate against, object to, protest against' is a loose rendering of 'S’emporter mal à propos pour une cause légère'; a later pass may prefer something like 'bristle, take umbrage'.
- **chourer** (4893): Colloquial verb; gloss has no (slang) label.
- **forclore** (4897): Infinitive used because audits show the app's present 3sg forclot versus Wiktionary forclôt; that disagreement and the dg 15 flag need a later look.
- **débriefer** (4901): Infinitive used: the app's model 1-4 apparently yields a form other than débriefe (likely débrièfe) for the present, which looks like an engine/model bug worth checking; débriefer should conjugate like a regular -er verb.
- **exonder** (4977): Pronominal sentence chosen because the app ships exonder as pronominal-only; the Gaudry candidate is a passive (ont été exondées).
- **créner** (5010): Dated type-founding sense only; no corpus evidence.
- **clochardiser** (5038): Intransitive use follows Wiktionnaire 'Devenir clochard'; the verb is commoner as pronominal or transitive.
- **zyeuter** (5098): Wiktionnaire calls zyeuter a spelling variant of zieuter; possible duplicate entry.
- **tringler** (5102): All candidates are the noun tringle. Gloss covers only the vulgar slang sense; the plain senses (equip with rods, snap a chalk line) are absent, worth a later pass.
- **démancher** (5126): Vallès candidate starts with dashes and is two sentences; Zola candidate is the reflexive 'contort' sense, not covered by the gloss.
- **invaginer** (5136): Pronominal form used because the app ships it as pronominal-only.
- **retapisser** (5137): Wiktionnaire's 'Tapisser à nouveau' also covers re-wallpapering; the gloss 'reupholster' is narrower.
- **désaliéner** (5149): Gloss 'de-alienate' is an unattested word; English Wiktionary has 'unalienate'. Consider a later pass.
- **tontiner** (5193): Wiktionnaire says 'Munir d’une tontine' (transitive); the gloss and English Wiktionary say 'participate in', so the sentence follows the gloss.
- **ululer** (5246): Whole sentence lifted from the Nicolardot (d. 1888) quotation.
- **crouter** (5299): Post-1990 spelling of croûter; duplicate gloss with croûter is expected.
- **infatuer** (5305): Candidate uses the participle (remain infatuated); Wiktionnaire tags that sense obsolete.
- **tapiner** (5317): Slang 'work' sense; English Wiktionary lists the prostitution sense first, so the gloss order may merit a later look.
- **estoquer** (5426): Wiktionnaire defines it as 'Donner l’estocade' (deliver the final thrust); the gloss 'impale' is looser.
- **dribler** (5445): Uses the app's spelling 'drible'; Wiktionnaire calls dribler a spelling variant of dribbler.
- **plucher** (5452): Wiktionnaire also lists a sense 'éplucher, faire des pluches' that the gloss does not cover; later pass may consider it.
- **désarrimer** (5540): The shipped gloss 'shift, displace' misses the core sense 'unfasten, cast loose'; consider a later pass.
- **briqueter** (5594): Past participle used to avoid the doubled-t/accent present-tense ambiguity.
- **guiper** (5654): Gloss 'wind, twist' is loose; the French senses are 'wrap a strand with thread/tape'.
- **houpper** (5794): Candidates only contain the noun houppe.
- **vacuoliser** (5818): Only reference sense is intransitive/reflexive (Se transformer avec apparition de vacuole); the app's re flag is false, which a later pass may want to revisit.
- **calter** (5823): Passé composé chosen to avoid the dg 5 defective forms. Feydeau candidate is a multi-line dialogue, rejected.
- **fourgonner** (5827): Second sentence lifted whole from the Boisgobey quotation (d. 1891).
- **périphraser** (5852): All candidates use the noun périphrase, rejected.
- **passementer** (5907): Quotation candidates use participles as adjectives or a metaphorical sense; authored a plainly verbal sentence.
- **cocoter** (5934): Spelling variant of cocotter.
- **cocotter** (5935): All candidates are the noun 'cocotte'.
- **bienvenir** (5971): All candidates are the adjective bienvenu. The verb is dated and survives mainly in se faire bienvenir, so the sentence uses that phrase.
- **dépaqueter** (5991): The nous form is the same in either spelling of the stem change, so the sentence avoids the dépaquette/dépaquète question; check the engine's third-person form.
- **terser** (6005): Variant spelling of tercer.
- **haricoter** (6023): Littré labels it 'Terme populaire'; a '(colloquial)' label could be considered. The Balzac candidate was not used because its context reads as the Normandy 'scrounge' sense.
- **moufeter** (6024): Moufeter is a variant spelling of moufter; the dg 15 flag is flagged by the audit as unsupported, for a later pass.
- **entabler** (6046): Wiktionnaire's senses (scissor blades, put on a table) differ from the horse sense in the gloss; for a later pass.
- **bêcheveter** (6241): Prior sentence used bêchevette, which the conjugator does not generate; infinitive used here. Possible engine disagreement for model 1-4.
- **ébiseler** (6270): Prior sentence used ébisèle, not matched by the app's conjugator; infinitive used.
- **tauder** (6321): Used without an object and in sun, not storm, per the skeptic's objections. Wiktionnaire tags the sense 'dated'; verb is nautical and rare.
